 Please note that this example ignores the effect of tax.

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698075992"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.85.2"></a>FAQ 48.85.2 – How are share-based awards, where the entity or the employee has a settlement choice, considered for diluted EPS purposes?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 47A  
 Reference to standing text: [48.85](79.md#s315-Para_48.85)  

  

  
 Whilst IAS 33 does not specifically cover the point if a contract may be settled in cash or shares at either the entity’s or the employee’s option, the more restrictive of the rules set out in [FAQ 48.98.1](79.md#s317-FAQ_48.98.1) (that is, where only the holder  has an option) should be applied. In practice, IAS 33 recognises that it is likely that only one party will have the option because, otherwise, there could be a conflict.

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698082904"></a>
#### Contingently issuable ordinary shares

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698091608"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.87.1"></a>FAQ 48.87.1 – How do contingently issuable ordinary shares arise?

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 5  
 Reference to standing text: [48.87](79.md#s315-Para_48.87)  

  

  
Often, acquisition agreements include a clause under which the purchaser of an acquired entity is required to transfer additional consideration in the form of ordinary shares in the future. The value of such shares might either be known precisely at the time of the acquisition, or it might be contingent upon the future performance or future evaluation of the acquired entity. The obligation might simply be deferred (deferred consideration), or the obligation might or might not arise, depending on whether or not certain earnings conditions are met (contingent consideration). In any event, the need to issue ordinary shares in future could lead to dilution of EPS. These are called ‘contingently issuable ordinary shares’.

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698091352"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.91.1"></a>FAQ 48.91.1 – How should diluted EPS reflect other types of contingently issuable share arrangements?

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 53  
 Reference to standing text: [48.91](79.md#s315-Para_48.91)  

  

  
The guiding principle is that, where the number of contingently issuable shares depends upon the level of earnings, the diluted EPS computation should include those shares to the extent that they would be issuable under the agreement, based on the current amount of earnings.

Earnings conditions in earn-out agreements come in various forms:

- Further shares might be issued if the average profit earned over a period is a specific amount.

- Maintenance of the current earnings level, or the attainment of a specified increased level of earnings of an acquired entity for a specified number of years, might be the condition.

- Conditions might specify the issue of shares when a minimum earnings target is reached, increasing rateably until the maximum earnings target is reached, with a cap on the maximum number of shares that could be issued.

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698097880"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.91.2"></a>FAQ 48.91.2 – Should contingently issuable shares based on average earnings be included in diluted EPS?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 53  
 Reference to standing text: [48.91](79.md#s315-Para_48.91)  

  

**Question**

  
On 1 January 20X4, entity A acquired the whole of entity B’s issued share capital. The total consideration payable in respect of the acquisition comprises initial consideration and deferred contingent consideration. Under the terms of the deferred contingent consideration, entity A is required to issue 100,000 shares if entity B’s profit for the year averages C100,000 over a three-year period. Any additional shares will be issued on 1 January 20X7, after the end of the three-year contingency period. Entity B’s profit for the year ended 31 December 20X4 amounted to C120,000.

Should the contingently issuable shares be included in the diluted EPS?

**Answer**

Given that the terms stipulate the achievement of C100,000 of average profit for the three-year period, it would appear at first sight that the contingency condition at the balance sheet date has been met, as the profit for the year ended 31 December 20X4 exceeds C100,000. This is not the case, however, because it assumes that the entity will earn profit averaging at C90,000 for each of the next two years ended 31 December 20X6. Projecting future earnings levels in this way is not permitted under IAS 33, because IAS 33 takes an historical approach and not a predictive or forward-looking approach in measuring dilution. The provisions relating to contingently issuable shares are quite specific and do not allow an entity to consider the probability of a contingent issue occurring.

The correct analysis is to measure whether performance achieved in the current period is deemed to be that achieved over the whole of the contingency period, as if the end of the reporting period was the end of the contingency period. On this basis, an average over a period has the same effect as if it were expressed as a cumulative amount over the period. So, in this situation, the contingency condition should be expressed in terms of a cumulative target of C300,000 over the three-year period. Since the profit for the year ended 31 December 20X4 is only C120,000, which is less than C300,000, the contingency condition is not met at the balance sheet, and no additional shares would be brought into the diluted EPS computation.

Similarly, if the profit for the year ended 31 December 20X5 were to increase to C150,000, again the contingency condition is not met in that year, because the cumulative earnings to date amount to C270,000. So, no additional shares would be included in that year. In the final year ended 31 December 20X6, when the contingency period comes to an end, the entity will know for certain whether or not the contingency conditions have been met. If the condition is met in that year, the entity will include 100,000 shares in both basic and diluted EPS.

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698118232"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.91.3"></a>FAQ 48.91.3 – Should contingently issuable shares based on a specified increased level of earnings be included in diluted EPS?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 53  
 Reference to standing text: [48.91](79.md#s315-Para_48.91)  

  

**Question**

  
The facts are the same as in [FAQ 48.91.2](79.md#s317-FAQ_48.91.2), except that the deferred contingent consideration agreement provides for the issue of 1,000 shares for each C1,000 of total profit in excess of C250,000 over the three years ending 31 December 20X6.

Should the contingently issuable shares be included in the diluted EPS?

**Answer**

Using the principles in paragraph [48.101](79.md#s315-Para_48.101), the entity did not earn C250,000 for the year ended 31 December 20X4. Again, projecting future earnings levels (C120,000 for 3 years = C360,000) and including 110,000 ((C360,000 − C250,000)/C1,000 × 1,000) contingent shares in the diluted EPS calculation is not permitted.

For the year ended 31 December 20X5, the cumulative amount earned to that date is C270,000. Because this amount exceeds C250,000, the contingency condition is met in that year, and the entity will include 20,000 contingently issuable shares in the diluted EPS calculation for that year. For the year ended 31 December 20X6, the cumulative amount earned to that date would be known, and the actual number of shares issued would be included in both basic and diluted EPS. If the actual number of shares amounts to, say, 50,000 shares, the prior year’s diluted EPS, which was based on 20,000 contingent shares, should not be restated.

Similar considerations apply when computing diluted EPS for interim reports. If, at 30 June 20X5, the cumulative amount earned to that date was C245,000 (C120,000 to 31 December 20X4 + C125,000 to 30 June 20X5), the contingency provision is not met. Therefore, no contingently issuable shares would be included in calculating the diluted EPS for the half year, even though, at the time of preparing the interim report, it is apparent that 20,000 shares will be included in the year-end diluted EPS calculation.

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698133080"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.93.1"></a>FAQ 48.93.1 – How are contingently issuable ordinary shares accounted for where the number of shares to be issued is unknown?

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 55  
 Reference to standing text: [48.93](79.md#s315-Para_48.93)  

  

  
In some deferred consideration agreements, the value of the deferred consideration is known, but the number of shares to be issued when the deferred consideration falls due is not known. IAS 33 does not specifically consider this situation. However, we consider that the number of shares to be included in the calculation should be based on the market price at the balance sheet date, as if it were the end of the contingency period.

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698144344"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.93.2"></a>FAQ 48.93.2 – How are contingently issuable ordinary shares accounted for where there are a number of different conditions?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 55  
 Reference to standing text: [48.93](79.md#s315-Para_48.93)  

  

  
 If the contingency is based on a number of different conditions, we consider that the determination of the number of shares included in diluted EPS should be based on the status of all relevant conditions, as they exist at the end of each reporting period. If one of the conditions is not met at the end of the reporting period, no contingently issuable shares should be included in diluted EPS. Although not specifically mentioned in IAS 33, it is implicit in the guidance described in [paragraph 48.93](79.md#s315-Para_48.93).

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698145880"></a>
#### Contracts that may be settled in ordinary shares or in cash

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698146136"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.97.1"></a>FAQ 48.97.1 – What is an example of a contract that may be settled in ordinary shares or in cash at the entity’s option?

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 59  
 Reference to standing text: [48.97](79.md#s315-Para_48.97)  

  

  
 An example of such a contract is a deferred or contingent consideration agreement, where the entity has the unrestricted right to settle the consideration in the form of ordinary shares or cash. At the date of acquisition, it might not be possible for the entity to determine how the deferred consideration will be settled. It should therefore be presumed that the contract will be settled in shares (that is, the more dilutive method), and the resulting potential ordinary shares will be included in diluted EPS in accordance with IAS 33’s relevant provisions.

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698154968"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.97.2"></a>FAQ 48.97.2 – How is EPS calculated with a convertible bond that the issuer can choose to settle in ordinary shares or cash?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 59, IAS 33 Example 8

  
 Reference to standing text: [48.97](79.md#s315-Para_48.97)  

  

**Question**

  
IAS 33 includes an example of the calculation of diluted EPS where the entity has in issue a convertible bond that may be settled in ordinary shares or in cash at the issuer’s option:

An entity issues 1,000 convertible bonds at the beginning of 20X5. The bonds have a three-year term and are issued at par with a face value of C1,000 per bond, giving total proceeds of C1,000,000. Interest is payable annually in arrears at a nominal annual interest rate of 4%. Each bond is convertible at any time up to maturity into 150 common shares. The entity has an option to settle the principal amount of the convertible bonds in ordinary shares or in cash.

When the bonds are issued, the prevailing market interest rate for similar debt without a conversion option is 9%. At the issue date, the market price of one common share is C3. Income tax is ignored.

<table style="width:100%; border-collapse:collapse"><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">Profit 20X5</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="text-align:right; background-color:transparent">C1,000,000</p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">Ordinary shares outstanding</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent"><b>Allocation of proceeds of the bond issue</b></p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">Liability component</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="text-align:right; background-color:transparent">C873,4341</p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">Equity component</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="text-align:right; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="text-align:right; background-color:transparent">C1,000,000</p></td></tr></table>

  
 Note 1: Present value of the principal and interest discounted at 9% – C1,000,000 payable at the end of three years; C40,000 payable annually in arrears for three years.

The liability and equity components would be determined in accordance with IAS 32. These amounts would be recognised as the initial carrying amounts of the liability and equity components presented on the balance sheet. The amount assigned to the issuer conversion option equity element is a permanent addition to equity and is not adjusted.

How are the earnings per share calculated?

**Answer**

Basic EPS 20X5:

<table style="width:100%; border-collapse:collapse"><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">C1,000,000</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">= C0.83 per ordinary share</p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">1,200,000</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td></tr></table>

  
 Diluted EPS 20X5:

It is presumed that the issuer will settle the contract by the issue of ordinary shares; the dilutive effect is calculated in accordance with paragraphs [48.52](79.md#s315-Para_48.52) (earnings) and [48.55](79.md#s315-Para_48.55) (number of shares).

<table style="width:100%; border-collapse:collapse"><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">C1,000,000 + C78,609<span style="font-size:6.67pt; vertical-align:super">1</span></p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">= C0.80 per ordinary share</p></td></tr><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">1,200,000 + 150,000<span style="font-size:6.67pt; vertical-align:super">2</span></p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td></tr></table>

  
 1 The earnings are adjusted for the interest recognised in the period on the convertible bond – that is, C78,609 (C873,434 × 9%).  
 2 150,000 ordinary shares = 150 ordinary shares × 1,000 convertible bonds

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698209112"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.98.1"></a>FAQ 48.98.1 – How are contracts, where settlement can occur using cash or shares at either the issuer’s or the holder’s option, considered for EPS purposes?

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 61  
 Reference to standing text: [48.98](79.md#s315-Para_48.98)  
 

  

  
 Whilst IAS 33 does not specifically cover the point, if a contract may be settled in cash or shares at either the issuer’s or the holder’s option, the more dilutive of the cash settlement and share settlement should be used in calculating diluted EPS (that is, the same approach as when only the holder has an option). In practice, the rules in IAS 33 recognise that it is likely that only one party will have the option because, otherwise, there could be a conflict.

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698204632"></a>
#### Written put options

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698218200"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.101.1"></a>FAQ 48.101.1 – How is EPS calculated with written put options?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 63  
 Reference to standing text: [48.101](79.md#s315-Para_48.101)  

  

**Question**

  
Assume that an entity has 160 written put options outstanding on 160 of its ordinary shares, with an exercise price of C10 per option. The put obligation is therefore C1,600. The average market price of the entity’s ordinary shares is C8 for the period.

How are the earnings per share calculated?

**Answer**

In calculating diluted EPS, the entity assumes that it issues 200 ordinary shares at C8 per share to raise the proceeds necessary to satisfy the put option. The difference between the 200 ordinary shares assumed to be issued and the 160 ordinary shares that would have been received on exercise of the option (that is, 40 shares) is added to the denominator (number of shares) in calculating the diluted EPS. No adjustments are made to the numerator (profit attributable to ordinary shareholders), because the shares are deemed issued for nil proceeds. This example is derived from that in paragraph A10 of IAS 33.

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698222808"></a>
#### Post balance sheet changes in capital

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698221400"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.103.1"></a>FAQ 48.103.1 – What post balance sheet changes in capital should be adjusted for?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 64  
 Reference to standing text: [48.103](79.md#s316-Para_48.103)  

  

  
 Post balance sheet changes in capital not described in [paragraph 48.103](79.md#s316-Para_48.103) (which describes bonus issues, share splits, share consolidations and other similar events) should not be adjusted for, but disclosure is required of ordinary share transactions or potential ordinary share transactions that would have changed significantly the number of ordinary shares or potential ordinary shares outstanding at the end of the period if they had occurred before the end of the period.

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_19"></a>
### Presentation and disclosure

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698228824"></a>
#### Presentation of basic and diluted EPS

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1698241496"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.105.1"></a>FAQ 48.105.1 – How could EPS be disclosed where there are no discontinued operations?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 68  
 Reference to standing text: [48.105](79.md#s316-Para_48.105)  

  

  
In practice, unless an entity has discontinued operations that it should disclose in accordance with IFRS 5, the EPS from continuing operations is likely to be identical to total EPS. Accordingly, if the basic EPS for continuing operations and total EPS is the same, and the diluted EPS is the same, the entity could disclose the figures as follows

If the basic and diluted figures are the same, the disclosure could be:

<table style="width:100%; border-collapse:collapse"><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="background-color:transparent">Basic and diluted EPS for profit from continuing operations and for profit for the year</p></td><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p style="text-align:right; background-color:transparent">x</p></td></tr></table>

 

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1697987032"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.106.1"></a>FAQ 48.106.1 – How could EPS be disclosed where there are discontinued operations?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 67  
 Reference to standing text: [48.106](79.md#s316-Para_48.106)  

  

  
Where an entity has  discontinued operations, its EPS from continuing operations will not be the same as its total EPS, and so disclosure of three basic and three diluted EPS figures will be required. Assuming that the EPS figures for profit or loss on discontinued operations are disclosed on the face of the statement of profit or loss and other comprehensive income, the disclosure might be as follows:

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1696046968"></a>
#### Additional disclosures

<a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1696045944"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.110.1"></a>FAQ 48.110.1 – How could the numerator and denominator be disclosed?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 70(a), (b), and (c)  
 Reference to standing text: [48.110](79.md#s316-Para_48.110)  

  

  
This example illustrates the basic and diluted EPS figures for the profit attributable to ordinary equity holders only, and does not illustrate the disclosure for continuing or discontinued operations.

<table style="width:100%; border-collapse:collapse"><tr><td style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; padding-right:5.03pt; padding-left:5.03pt; vertical-align:top"><p> </p></td><td colspan="4" style="border:0.75pt solid #000000; <a id="s317-chap_48_navPoint_idp1696109304"></a>
##### <a id="s317-FAQ_48.119.1"></a>FAQ 48.119.1 – What should be considered when publishing a five-year track record for EPS purposes?

 

Reference to standard: IAS 33 para 26  
 Reference to standing text: [48.119](79.md#s316-Para_48.119)  

  

  
 Entities often publish a historical summary, usually covering at least five years. IAS 33 does not deal specifically with adjustments to historical summaries, but the following guidance is relevant in such situations. In order to present a fair comparison of EPS figures published in such a summary, the basic EPS figure will need to be adjusted for subsequent changes in capital, as set out below:

- Where a capitalisation issue or share split has taken place during a financial year, all previously published EPS figures should be adjusted by the bonus factor (see further [para 48.40](79.md#s314-Para_48.40)).

- Where a rights issue at less than full market has taken place during a financial year, all previously published EPS figures should be adjusted by the reciprocal of the bonus element inherent in the rights issue (see further [para 48.41](79.md#s314-Para_48.41)).

Where there is more than one capitalisation or rights issue during the year, both of these factors will operate cumulatively. The cumulative effect of all of these events should be taken into account. The resultant figures should be described as restated EPS, and they should be set out separately from the other financial data that is not so adjusted.

Where there has been a bonus or rights issue in the period covered by the summary, the ordinary dividend actually paid in those periods should be set out in the form of pence per share, and it should be similarly adjusted by the same factors used in restating EPS. This adjustment is necessary to ensure that the ordinary dividends and EPS data are comparable. The adjusted dividend per share should be described as restated. In practice, the adjusted EPS and the adjusted dividend per share are normally presented next to each other.
